- fields: a declared star, scale factor or dust cloud against the published Einstein tensor, the neutron star against $G^\theta{}_\theta = 8\pi p$, FRW's and Oppenheimer-Snyder's scale factors against their $G^r{}_r$ and $G^\chi{}_\chi$, each cloud released from rest against Tolman-Bondi's $G^r{}_r = 0$ and its $G^t{}_t$, the density, and Taub's scale factors against every published Einstein component of the Mixmaster universe; the pp-wave's and the Aichelburg-Sexl shock's particles are run with their published Christoffel symbols, Kasner's and Bianchi's are held at rest by theirs, and the length of the Malament-Hogarth tube is checked against the proper time of the computer on its axis;
- marks: every curve and point marked on a surface lies on its piece, at the height the profile has at its distance from the axis or on the triangles of a grid, and each of Natário's lines of flow closes and holds Stokes's stream function to one value;
- stops: where a view or a file says a slice cannot be drawn, $g_{xx} - (d\rho/dx)^2$ is negative at every sample, or $g_{\phi\phi}$ is, where the circles are timelike; where it says a slice is a plane, it is zero, or the spatial metric has no cross term and no component that depends on a spatial coordinate.

On 28 September 2026 the worst chord missed its proper distance by $1.0 \times 10^{-4}$ of it, a chord $9 \times 10^{-4}$ long on the lip of the Malament-Hogarth well at $ct = -0.3$, where the rounding is most of it, and the worst line across by $1.1 \times 10^{-4}$, on the sphere of radius $a = 1/2$ of FRW's first moment, for the same reason.
Every profile end to end was within $3.6 \times 10^{-5}$ of its proper length, every $\rho$ within $2.5 \times 10^{-8}$ of the drawing's size of $\sqrt{g_{\phi\phi}}$, every closed form within $2.5 \times 10^{-8}$ of the size, every join met to $3 \times 10^{-13}$ with tangents equal to $3 \times 10^{-14}$, and every declared solution satisfied the published field equations to $1.4 \times 10^{-14}$, Taub's exactly; the triangles of Alcubierre's and the Krasnikov tube's grids lay within $4.9 \times 10^{-4}$ and $4.2 \times 10^{-4}$ of the size of their heights, in space; 645 checks in all, none failing, and running the script again writes the same bytes.
On 30 September 2026, with every frame of the movies checked as a moment is, it made 3854 checks, none failing, and the worst chord missed its proper distance by $1.4 \times 10^{-4}$ of it.
The tests hold the files on disk to the same closed forms without sympy, from the numbers written and nothing else, so a redraw that changed a surface fails there too.

### Which spacetimes

`DRAWN` in the script names every spacetime, and `STATED` and `NOT_DRAWN` are empty: a spacetime put in `STATED` would have a file with no surface that says why, checked from the published metric before it is said, and one in `NOT_DRAWN` no file, and the script stops if a metric file is in none of the three or in more than one.
Minkowski space is its equator from the spherical chart, the plane every other diagram is measured against, out to $r = 4\ell$ in any length $\ell$, and every slice of constant $t$ of its Cartesian chart is checked flat.
Hotta and Tanaka's shock is the sphere of constant $u$ and $v$ of its Kruskal chart through a ring of free particles, at $8GE/c^4 = a$, played as a movie in the particles' proper time from $\tau = -0.6\,a$ to $0.5\,a$ with a frame every $0.025\,a$: the ring $\theta = 30°$ of the dust at rest in the closed slicing on the great sphere $u = v$ is run with the published Christoffel symbols and a pulse of width $0.005$, each sphere is drawn as two hemispheres checked against $R\cos\theta$, and the run is checked against Podolský and Ortaggio's geodesics, by which the ring closes on the axis at $\sinh(\tau/a) = 1/\sqrt{3}$; its moments are events on the planes of the Kruskal, global and null cylindrical charts and of the conformally flat chart near a particle, and lie on no drawing of the Kundt chart or of the conformally flat chart's equator, which `HIDDEN` records.
Misner space is its contracting region at $ct = -2$, $-1.5$, $-1$ and $-0.5$ in the Milne chart at $\psi_0 = 4\pi$, where $\chi$ runs once round $2\pi$: each moment's slice $z = 0$ is a flat cylinder of radius $c|t|$, checked against $\rho = c|t|$ and $z = y$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.05\,\ell$ of $ct$, and its moments lie on the Misner and Milne planes and on none of the Rindler region's drawings, which `HIDDEN` records.
Gott's time machine is the slice $z = 0$ of Grant's Milne chart at $c\tau = -2$, $-1$, $-0.5$ and $-0.25\,\ell$, away from the strings and to the past of the chronology horizon, for the strings its other diagrams declare: each moment is a flat cylinder of circumference $\sqrt{a^2c^2\tau^2 + b^2}$, which `Slice` reaches by a `swept` that moves both $\chi$ and $Y$ with the angle and the length, so that the circle closes after the shift $b$ and the pulled back metric has no cross term; each radius is checked against $\sqrt{a^2c^2\tau^2 + b^2}/2\pi$, which stays above $b/2\pi$, the moments are played as a movie with a frame every $0.05\,\ell$ of $c\tau$, and they lie on the Milne plane and on neither the Rindler region's drawings nor the figure about the strings, which `HIDDEN` records.
Ori's time machine is the slice $y = 0$ of his surfaces of constant $t$ at $t = -2$, $-1$, $-0.5$ and $-0.1\,\ell^2$, for the example its other diagrams declare, played as a movie with a frame every $0.05\,\ell^2$: with $z = \phi - \tfrac{2e - a}{2e}\ln(ex^2 - t)$, a `swept` that turns each circle round itself, the cross term goes and each moment is a throat of radius $\sqrt{ex^2 - t}$, $\rho$ checked, which narrows toward $t = 0$, where its central circle is the closed null geodesic $N$.
A moment of the centre of momentum time through both strings is a flat sheet with two conical points, no surface of revolution, so it is left to the figure of the closed timelike curve.
Gowdy's torus universe is the torus of $\theta$ and $\sigma$ at $\delta = 0$ for the wave its other diagrams declare, a movie in the areal time from $t = 1/4$ to $3$: each moment is a surface of revolution about an axis along $\theta$, a tube whose two ends are one circle, with the radius checked against $L\sqrt{t}\,e^{P/2}$ and the wave against the published Einstein tensor; its moments lie on both planes of the torus and on neither drawing of the sphere chart, which `HIDDEN` records.
The wormhole time machine is the plane $\theta = \pi/2$ of Morris, Thorne and Yurtsever's chart at one moment of $t$, with the wormhole its spacetime diagrams declare: the catenoid $z = r_0\,\mathrm{arcsinh}(l/r_0)$ from $l = -4\,r_0$ through the throat to $4\,r_0$, checked against that closed form and against $\rho = \sqrt{r_0^2 + l^2}$, and checked to be the same surface at the trip's greatest acceleration and deceleration, since neither $g$ nor $F$ enters where $\cos\theta = 0$; it lies on none of the spacetime's other drawings, which `HIDDEN` records.
Anti-de Sitter's static equator has $g_{rr} < (d\rho/dr)^2$ at every $r > 0$, which is checked, so it is drawn in Minkowski space: the sheet $Z = \sqrt{L^2 + r^2} - L$ of a hyperboloid out to $r = 4L$, with the light cone it nears drawn as a reference piece, $Z = \rho - L$, given by its closed form since it is no slice of the spacetime.
The Malament-Hogarth plane $z = 0$, with the $\Omega$ its spacetime diagram declares, is turned about the removed event at $ct = -0.7$, $-0.3$, $-0.1$ and $0$, and played as a movie with a frame every $1/80$ of $ct$ up to the last whose well is no deeper than the tube at $ct = 0$, since the wells just before it reach deeper than the tube is drawn, the flat plane at $z = 0$ in every frame: a well inside the unit ball, flat beyond, and at $ct = 0$ a funnel into a tube of radius $1$ drawn down to $s = 0.03$, whose length from the rim down to $s$ is checked to be the proper time of the computer on the axis from $ct = -1$ to $-s$; the edge of the region where $\Omega > 1$ is $\sqrt{1 - c^2t^2}$ to the last digit, moved on by a unit in the last place where the declared function's two branches disagree within one of it, as they do at $ct = -0.7$.
The Mixmaster universe is the great two sphere of its three sphere, $\psi = -\phi$ and $\psi = 2\pi - \phi$ in the Euler angles, at five moments of Abraham Taub's universe, played as a movie with a frame about every $0.05\,m$ of $c\tau$, each at the $T$ whose proper time it is, $a_1 = a_2$, at $m = 1$ and $l = m/2$ as Taub-NUT declares: only there does the great sphere have an axis, through the identity, and where $a_3 > 2a_1/\sqrt{3}$ its curvature about the poles is negative and the drawing is the band about the equator, which the view states and the script checks.
Kasner's universe, Bianchi type I and the pp-wave are each a flat plane at four moments, Kasner's and Bianchi's the plane $y = 0$ at the exponents and the dust their spacetime diagrams declare, the pp-wave's its wave front at $cu = -3$, $-0.5$, $0$ and $0.661\,L$ with the pulse $A = e^{-u^2}$ its spacetime diagram declares, each with a ring of free particles marked on it as a curve and twelve of them as points: at rest in the chart for Kasner and Bianchi, and run from rest before the pulse for the pp-wave, which focuses every one onto the $x$ axis at once.
The Aichelburg-Sexl shock is its flat wave front at $u = -1$, $0.5$, $1$ and $1.5$ in units of $8GE/c^4$, with a ring of free particles at rest about the axis before the shock, run with the published Christoffel symbols through a declared pulse of width $0.005$ in place of the delta, and drawn first as the ring's world tube: the ring stays a circle, of radius $1 - u/2$ behind the shock, and closes on the axis at $u = 2$, as `_tools/derivations/aichelburg_sexl.md` derives.
Bonnor's beam of light is its flat wave front at $u = 0$, $2$, $4$, $6$, $8$ and $10$ in units of the beam's radius, with the beam's edge marked and a ring of free particles at rest on $\rho = 2R$ at $u = 0$, run with the published Christoffel symbols of the null Cartesian chart and drawn first as the ring's world tube: the ring stays a circle, reaches the edge at $u = 2\sqrt{2\pi}\,\mathrm{erf}(\sqrt{\ln 2})\,R = 3.81\,R$, closes on the axis at $5.22\,R$ and is back at rest on its first circle at $10.45\,R$, as `_tools/derivations/light_beam.md` derives.
Bach and Weyl's ring is the plane $z = 0$ of Weyl's chart at $t = 0$ and $m = a/2$, in two views: outside the ring a funnel in flat space with a neck of radius $2.075\,a$ at $\rho = 1.220\,a$, drawn from $\rho = 1.116\,a$, inside which the circles grow faster than the distance in to them, out to $4\,a$; and the disc inside the ring, where every circle out to $\rho = 0.993\,a$ grows faster than the distance out to it, drawn in Minkowski space from the axis to $\rho = 0.98\,a$. The oblate spheroidal chart is checked to give the same circles, and `_tools/derivations/bach_weyl_ring.md` Step 6 has the numbers.
Bonnor's stars of charged dust are four views of the moment $t = 0$ at $m = 1$: the equator of each sphere at $r_0 = 2m$, a bowl with $\rho = rU$ and $dz/dr = \sqrt{-r\,\partial_rU(2U + r\,\partial_rU)}$, checked by quadrature, joined with one tangent to Majumdar and Papapetrou's throat cut at $\rho = 3m$; the equator of Lemos and Weinberg's cloud at $b = m/2$; and the equatorial plane of the spheroid at $a = m$ and $u_0 = 1$, in three pieces, the disc $u = 0$ inside the focal ring, read along $\theta$ and checked flat, then $\theta = 0$ from the ring through the surface $u_0$ and on outside.
The black holes of string theory with three and four charges are five views of the moment $t = 0$, one for each chart, on the plane of the radius and $\phi$: off extremality two sheets joined at the bifurcation sphere, of circumference radius $\prod(r_0^2 + r_i^2)^{1/6}$ in five dimensions and $\prod(r_0 + r_i)^{1/4}$ in four; and for each extreme hole a throat without end, drawn from $r = r_2/50$, whose circles close on $(r_1r_2r_3)^{1/3}$ and $(r_1r_2r_3r_4)^{1/4}$.
The Khan-Penrose spacetime is its flat wave front at the four events $\tau = 0$, $0.6$, $1$ and $1.3$ on $\sigma = 0$ of the cosmological chart, with a ring of free particles at rest, the ellipse of semi-axes $(1 \pm \sin\tau)\ell/\sqrt{\cos\tau}$, whose area $\pi\ell^2\cos\tau$ vanishes at the singularity.
Belinski and Zakharov's wave of two solitons is its flat plane of $x$ and $y$ at the four events $\tau = 0.25$, $0.5$, $1$ and $2$ on $\xi = 0$ of the pole chart, midway between the two pulses, at $w = 1$ and $\cosh\beta = 5/4$, with a ring of free particles at rest, the ellipse of semi-axes $\sqrt{g_{xx}}\,\ell$ and $\sqrt{g_{yy}}\,\ell$, whose area is $\pi\ell^2\sinh\tau$ and whose axes are in the ratio $(\cosh\beta\cosh\tau + 1)/(\cosh\beta\cosh\tau - 1)$; it is drawn as the ring's world tube and as a movie with a frame every $0.05$ of $\tau$.
The Bell-Szekeres spacetime is its flat wave front at the four events $\xi = 0$, $0.5$, $0.9$ and $1.2$ on $\eta = 0$ of the chart of $\xi$ and $\eta$, with a ring of free particles at rest, the ellipse of semi-axes $\ell$ and $\ell\cos\xi$, whose area $\pi\ell^2\cos\xi$ vanishes at the Killing-Cauchy horizon, drawn first as the ring's world tube at $3\,\ell$ for each unit of $\xi$, with the flat view played as a movie, a frame every $0.025$ of $\xi$.
The Chandrasekhar-Xanthopoulos waves are their flat wave front at the four events $\psi = 0$, $0.5$, $0.9$ and $1.2$ on $\lambda = 0$ of the chart of $\psi$ and $\lambda$, at $p = 3/5$ and $q = 4/5$, with a ring of free particles at rest, drawn first as the ring's world tube at $3\,\ell$ for each unit of $\psi$, with the flat view played as a movie, a frame every $0.025$ of $\psi$. The front's metric carries a cross term, since the polarizations of the waves are out of line, so the ring's ellipse has no fixed axes and which way it is drawn has to be chosen: `FlatPlane` takes a `frame` $E$ with $E^TE$ the plane's metric, checked, and draws through it, and the frame handed over is the one parallel transport carries along the world line of the ring's centre, $dE_{ia}/d\psi = E_{ib}\Gamma^b{}_{\psi a}$ with the published Christoffel symbols, from the identity at the collision. In it the ring is stretched, squeezed and turned, its area $\pi\ell^2\cos\psi$, its long axis swinging through $63°$ by the horizon, where it closes onto a segment of half length $2\sqrt2\,\ell$. A stack of such rings is a stack of sheared ellipses: a row gives `sx` and `sy` beside `a` and `b` and is the ellipse $(a\cos v + s_x\sin v,\; s_y\cos v + b\sin v)$, the image of the unit circle under the frame, its columns still the ring's particles; `EllipsesPiece` writes the two only where a row has any, `check_ellipses` in `build_mfs_data.py` and `gridPiece` in `MFS/assets/turn.js` read them, and `_tools/test_chandrasekhar_xanthopoulos.py` holds the tube to the front's metric and to parallel transport.
Each is drawn first as the ring's world tube, as the captain asked on 30 September 2026, and the flat moments are the view beside it, played as a movie as the captain asked on 1 October 2026, with a frame every $0.05$ of Kasner's $t$, of Bianchi's $c\bar Ht$ and of the shock's $u$, every $0.025$ of the Khan-Penrose $\tau$ and every $0.1\,L$ of the wave's $cu$, `ring_moments()` and `ring_movie()` in the script; the tube is the ring at every time from the first moment to the last, each ellipse at the height of its time, $1.5\,\ell$ for a unit of Kasner's $t$, $2.5\,\ell$ for $1/\bar H$ of Bianchi's, $L/2$ for each $L$ of the wave's $cu$ and $3\,\ell$ for each unit of the Khan-Penrose $\tau$, as the view's `height` says.
`stack()` in the script builds it as a stack of ellipses whose columns are the ring's particles, one a degree, and checks every row against the ring at its time, from the published metric for Kasner and Bianchi and from the particles run with the published Christoffel symbols for the wave, and the tube's rows at the four moments against the flat views' rings.
The Krasnikov tube is $1 - k$, twice the published $g_{tx}$, as a height over the plane of its axis at $ct = 5$, a unit after the ship reached the far end of the declared tube, when the whole tube stands as a ridge along the path: over $-1 \le x \le 5$, as its spacetime diagram runs, and out to $r = 2$ on either side, on a Cartesian grid with its lines every $\rho_0/2$, with the path from $x = 0$ to $D$ and the level line $k = 0$, where the published $g_{xx}$ vanishes, marked as `wall`; `_tools/derivations/krasnikov_height.md` is the derivation.
Alcubierre's drive is the expansion $\theta = v_s\,\partial_xf$ of the observers who ride its flat slices, read from the published metric, as the height $\theta R^2/4c$ over the plane $z = 0$ of the path at $t = 0$, the ship heading along the drawing's $X$, on a polar grid of 72 angles with its circles every $R/4$: marked on it, the path, the circle $v_sf = 1$, the zero of the published $g_{tt}$, as `wall`, and the level lines where $\theta$ is half its greatest value, `contract` ahead of the ship and `expand` behind it; `_tools/derivations/alcubierre_expansion.md` is the derivation.
Teo's wormhole has two views at $b_0 = 1$: its equatorial plane, Flamm's paraboloid $z = \pm 2\sqrt{b_0(r - b_0)}$ for every spin, with the ergosurface $r = \sqrt{2}\,b_0$ of $a = 1$ marked on either side and the proper distance chart checked to give the same surface, and its throat $r = b_0$ at $a = 1/4$, the closed surface of $\theta$ and $\phi$ whose circles have radius $b_0(1 + \cos^2\theta)\sin\theta$, Teo's dumbbell; the equator's moment is marked on the equator's drawings and the throat's, where its poles meet the axis, on the axis's.
Natário's drive is the energy density $\varepsilon = -c^4K_{ij}K^{ij}/16\pi G$ of the observers who ride its flat slices, read from the published $G^{tt}$ with the declared field and checked against $-K_{ij}K^{ij}/2$ from the published shift, whose trace, the expansion, is checked to vanish, as the height $G^{tt}R^2/16$ over the plane $z = 0$ of the path at $t = 0$, the ship heading along the drawing's $X$ as Alcubierre's does, on a polar grid of 72 angles with its circles every $R/4$: a moat in the wall, deepest beside the ship at $r_s = 0.88R$, and level at the ship and far outside.
Marked on it, the path, the circle $r_s = R$ as `wall`, and six lines of the declared flow, each followed from the ship's plane $x = 0$ round to it again and set on the triangles of the grid.
Schwarzschild is Flamm's paraboloid on both sheets of the Einstein-Rosen bridge, the slice of constant $t$ running through the bifurcation sphere into the other exterior.
The interior Schwarzschild star, at $R = 1.5\,r_s$ as its conformal diagram declares, is the cap of a sphere of radius $\sqrt{R^3/r_s}$ joined to the exterior of `schwarzschild.json`, which the file reads, with the vacuum paraboloid drawn on under the cap down to the throat the star replaces.
The Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff star is the polytrope its other diagrams declare, $M = 1.40\,M_\odot$ and $R = 14.2$ km, with the mass function `null_rays.StarSolver` solves from the published Einstein tensor entering the slice as numbers; its exterior is checked to be Flamm's paraboloid of that mass, and the vacuum paraboloid is drawn under it as under Schwarzschild's star.
The boson star is one smooth sheet with no surface, drawn in two pieces that meet with one tangent on the sphere holding 99% of its mass, with $g_{rr} = a^2$ entering the slice as numbers from `boson_star.Star`; its slope at the rim is checked to be Flamm's for the star's mass, the vacuum paraboloid of that mass is drawn under it down to its throat, and its isotropic chart is checked to give the same surface.
Einstein's cluster has three views, each joined where it has a surface to the exterior of `schwarzschild.json` at $R = 3\,r_s$: his own at $V = 1/2$, whose slice is a cone, checked against $z = \sqrt{2}\,Vr$; Florides's of uniform density, the cap of a sphere of radius $\sqrt{R^3/r_s}$; and the declared cluster with no surface of the areal chart, checked to have $g_{rr} = r/(r - 2m)$ with the mass function its redshift function defines and $3m < r$, with the vacuum paraboloid of its whole mass drawn beside it.
Bartnik and McKinnon's soliton has a view for each of its first three solitons, with one, two and three zeros of $w$, in units of $\ell$: the areal chart with the solver's mass function, $dz/dr = \sqrt{2m/(r - 2m)}$, checked against $g_{rr} = r/(r - 2m)$ and against the field equations, with the vacuum paraboloid of the same mass beside it, set to meet it at $8\,\ell$, and the innermost circle on which $w = 0$ marked. The neck steepens from a slope of $1.77$ to $5.2$ and $18.3$ as $1 - 2m/r$ falls to $0.242$, $0.035$ and $0.0030$. Only the first soliton's moment lies on the spacetime diagrams and the conformal diagram, which draw that soliton, as `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
Visser's thin shell wormhole, at $a = 5r_s/4$, is Flamm's paraboloid on either side from the throat out to $5\,r_s$, the two sides meeting at the throat with a crease, each leaving it at $dz/d\rho = \sqrt{r_s/(a - r_s)} = 2$, which the script checks in place of a common tangent; its chart through the throat is checked to give the same surface.
Morris-Thorne is drawn as its Ellis-Bronnikov member, $b = b_0^2/r$, the catenoid, as its other diagrams declare; the surface reads $b$ alone, and its proper radial chart, with $r(l) = \sqrt{l^2 + b_0^2}$, is checked to give the same surface.
Reissner-Nordstrom, at $r_q = 0.48\,r_s$ as its conformal diagram declares, has two views: outside $r_+$ the two sheets through the outer horizon's bifurcation sphere, and inside $r_-$ the two sides through the inner one, its widest circle there, each running in until $g_{rr} = 1$ at $r = r_q^2/r_s$, where the surface lies level; nearer the singularity and between the horizons no surface carries the slice, which the views state under `stops` and the script checks.
Bardeen's regular black hole, at $g = r_s/3$, has the same two views: outside $r_+$ the two sheets through the outer bifurcation sphere, and inside $r_-$ two caps that meet on the inner one and close smoothly over the two centres, a closed surface, with the cap's curvature at the centre checked against the sphere of radius $\sqrt{g^3/r_s}$; its $g_{rr}$ holds a square root, so `Slice.horizons` takes the zeros from the resultant that clears it and `Slice._local` evaluates the metric beside a horizon in sixty digits.
Kastor and Traschen's holes have two views, each belonging to every chart: one hole's equator at $H\tau = 1/2$ in the isotropic chart, $m = 1$ and $H = -3c/(16m)$, from $r = m/25$ to $8m$, Majumdar and Papapetrou's throat over the areal radius $r/2 + m$, checked against $z = 2w + \ln((w - 1)/(w + 1))$ with $w = \sqrt{r/m + 1}$ and marked where it crosses the horizons $R = 4m/3$ and $4m$; and the midplane $z = 0$ of two holes at $z = \pm 2m$ in the cylindrical chart, $H = -3c/(32m)$, at $c\tau = -8$, $-6.1995$, $-3$ and $-1\,m$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.2\,m$, each checked against $\rho U$, with the circle where the last ray of the plane to reach infinity stands marked from the moment the horizons join. `Slice` writes a name its chart defines and holds as a function, as $U$, out before it reads the metric, unless the row gives a function for it.
The BTZ black hole is its moment $t = 0$ without rotation, $M = 1$ and $J = 0$, as its conformal diagram's square draws it, in one view through the throat $r_+ = \ell$ into both exteriors out to $r = 3\ell$: in flat space out to $r = \sqrt{2}\,\ell$, where $N^2 = 1$ and the surface lies level, and beyond that in Minkowski space, where it climbs from level toward a light cone as anti-de Sitter space's hyperboloid does.
The quantum BTZ black hole is its moment $t = 0$ at $M = F = 1/4$ and $\ell = 15\ell_3/16$, in one view through the throat $r_+ = 3\ell_3/4$, in flat space out to the circle where $H = 1$, at the root $1.202\,\ell_3$ of $64r^3 - 80r - 15$, which `quantum_btz` names exactly for the slope there, and in Minkowski space beyond, as the BTZ hole's is.
The Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter black hole is drawn the same way at $r_s = 2L$: its moment $t = 0$ through the throat $r_h = L$ into both exteriors out to $r = 3L$, in flat space out to $r = (r_sL^2)^{1/3} = 1.26\,L$, where $g_{rr} = 1$, and in Minkowski space beyond, with the same checks.
The topological black holes have two views: the black string's moment $t = 0$, $z = 0$ at $\mu = L$, drawn as Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter's is, in flat space from the throat $r_h = L$ out to the root $1.3247\,L$ of $r^3 - L^2r - \mu L^2$, where $g_{rr} = 1$, and in Minkowski space beyond to $3L$; and the horizon of the hyperbolic hole without mass at one moment, the hyperbolic plane of curvature $-1/L^2$, the sheet $Z = L\cosh\theta - L$ of a hyperboloid in Minkowski space, drawn to $\theta = 2$ with its light cone.
The anti-de Sitter soliton is the surface of $\rho$ and $\phi$ of its polar chart at one moment and one $x$, at $r_0 = L$, from the tip to $\rho = 2L$: its circles outgrow the distance out to them at every $\rho > 0$, so it is drawn in Minkowski space, level at the tip, where its curvature $-\tanh^2(3\rho/2L)/L^2$ vanishes, with the light cone it nears, whose apex is $1.23\,L$ below the tip.
The topological star has two views at $r_B = 1$ and $r_S = 3/4$: the cigar, the surface of $\rho$ and $\psi$ of the chart about the bubble out to $\rho = 8$, whose circles have the radius $4\rho/\sqrt{16 + \rho^2}$ and whose slope against the proper distance falls from $1$ on the bubble, so flat space carries it; and the equator, the surface of $r$ and $\phi$ of Bah and Heidmann's chart at $y = 0$ and $y = \pi R_y$, joined along the bubble's equator, each height checked against the quadrature of its slope.
Siklos's waves have one view, a wave front: the surface $u = v = 0$ of the chart on the disc, $(d\xi^2 + d\eta^2)/p^2$ whatever the profile, turned about the centre of the disc as the Malament-Hogarth plane is turned, the sheet $Z = L\cosh(s/L) - L$ of a hyperboloid in Minkowski space drawn to a proper distance $s = 2L$ with its light cone, and on it the lines $x = L/2$, $L$ and $2L$ of Siklos's chart, horocycles, each checked to keep $p/q = x/L$ and to have chords equal to the arcs $L\,dy/x$ of Siklos's published metric.
Schrödinger spacetime has one view, under every chart: the surface $t = 0$, $\xi = 0$, the hyperbolic plane $(L^2/r^2)(dx^2 + dr^2)$ at every $\beta$, swept about its point $x = 0$, $r = L$ by a `swept` that carries the half plane onto the unit disc, $x + ir = iL(1 + \zeta)/(1 - \zeta)$, with the half line $x = 0$, $r \ge L$ for its profile; `Slice` clears the angle from the pulled back metric by writing $\sin^2$ as $1 - \cos^2$ where `simplify` leaves it, `_without_angle`. It is the sheet $Z = L\cosh(s/L) - L$ with $s = L\ln(r/L)$, drawn to $s = 2L$ with its light cone and the horocycles $r = L/2$, $L$ and $2L$, each checked against the arcs $L\,dx/r$ of the published metric.
Kundt's waves have two views, under the chart with a cosmological constant: the wave front $u = v = 0$ in de Sitter space, at $\alpha = 1$, $\beta = 0$ and $\Lambda = 3/\ell^2$, the hemisphere $q > 0$ of a sphere of radius $\ell$ with the envelope $q = 0$ its equator, and the surface $u = v = 0$ in anti-de Sitter space, at $\alpha = 0$, $\beta = 1/\ell$ and $\Lambda = -3/\ell^2$, the sheet of Siklos's front with the envelope $\xi = 0$, a geodesic through the centre, and the lines $q/p = 1/2$, $1$ and $2$ on the half $\xi > 0$ that is the front, each checked to keep its $q/p$ and to lie on the sheet. With no cosmological constant the front is a flat half plane and has no view.
Kerr-de Sitter is the equatorial slice of constant $t$ of Carter's chart for each sign of $\Lambda$, its distance from the axis the circumference radius $\sqrt{g_{\phi\phi}}$, which on a horizon is $(r^2 + a^2)/(\Xi r)$: for $\Lambda > 0$ from the throat $r_+$ to the widest circle $r_c$ and on into the next region, as Kottler's, with both ergosurfaces marked, and for $\Lambda < 0$ in flat space out to the circle where $g_{rr} = (d\rho/dr)^2$ and in Minkowski space beyond, as Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter's. That circle is a root of a polynomial of the tenth degree, and `Slice.slope` evaluates the tangent at such a root to forty digits, where sympy's limit did not finish.
The spinning string, at $b = 0.9$ and $a = 0.9\,r_c$, is its moment $t = 0$ outside $r_c$, read in the circumference radius chart: in Minkowski space from the axis, which it leaves along the light cone, out to $r = r_c/\sqrt{1 - b^2} = 2.29\,r_c$, where $g_{RR} = 1$ and it lies level, and in flat space beyond, out to $5\,r_c$, climbing toward the slope $\sqrt{1/b^2 - 1}$ of the cosmic string's cone, which is checked; `Piece` takes `knots`, further points of the profile, since near the light cone a chord of Minkowski space is longer than its arc however the profile looks on the page, and `Slice.known` names an irrational join exactly.
The point particle in three dimensions, at $\alpha = 3/4$, is its moment $t = 0$, the cone of half angle $\arcsin\alpha$ with Gott and Alpert's planet a cap at its apex, laid flat beside it with a quarter missing, and the ideal particle's cone unrolling onto the plane, the cosmic string's movie in steps of $2°$; the circumference radius and isotropic charts are checked to carry the same circles.
The travelling wave on a string, at $b = 1/2$, is the surface of constant $u$ and $v$, the cone of half angle $30°$ at every $u$, with a ring of 360 free particles at rest on $r = 2\ell$ before the declared pulse, played as a movie in $u$: `string_wave_ring` runs the particles with the published Christoffel symbols of the null conical chart and again with those of the isotropic chart, and the two rings are checked to be one; the ring crosses the cone as the string swings out and back and is left falling toward the string, which `StringWaveRing` in the tests holds the file to with an integration of its own.
The throat of extreme Kerr has two views at $r_0 = 1$, read in the global chart: its equator at the moment $\tau = 0$, the cylinder of radius $\sqrt{2}\,r_0$ with $z = r_0\,\mathrm{arsinh}(y)/\sqrt{2}$, drawn from $y = -2.25$ to $2.25$, and the sphere of $\theta$ and $\phi$ at one event, the horizon of the extreme hole, in flat space within $17.19°$ of the equator and in Minkowski space from there to each pole, level on the circles $\theta = 72.81°$ and $107.19°$ where $\cos^3\theta + \cos^2\theta + 3\cos\theta = \pm 1$; `LevelSlice` states the tangent there exactly, and the Gaussian curvature $4(1 - 3\cos^2\theta)/r_0^2(1 + \cos^2\theta)^3$ and the meridian's length $2.70\,r_0$ are checked from the published metric.
Senovilla's universe is the plane $z = 0$ of a moment of $t$ at $a = 1$, a movie from $act = -1$ to $1$ with a frame every $0.05$: the circle at $\rho$ has radius $\cosh^2(act)\sinh(3a\rho)/3a\cosh^{1/3}(3a\rho)$, $\rho$ checked, every moment is checked to embed whole and to be the moment of the bounce enlarged by $\cosh^2(act)$, and the circle of the one circular light ray is marked.
Each exterior is two pieces, which meet in the ring of class `space`; the script checks that flat space carries no part beyond $\sqrt{2}\,\ell$ and Minkowski space none inside it, that the two parts of each exterior meet in one circle with one tangent, in the numbers and in the file, and that the two exteriors meet at the throat.
The rotating hole's diagrams list the moment in `slices.HIDDEN`, since their hole is another spacetime.
Kerr, at $a = 0.9\,GM/c^2$, and Kerr-Newman, at $a = 0.6\,GM/c^2$ and $r_Q = 0.5\,GM/c^2$, as their conformal diagrams declare, are the equator of a slice of constant Boyer-Lindquist $t$, where $g_{t\phi}$ drops out, drawn at the circumference radius $\rho = \sqrt{g_{\phi\phi}}$ through the bifurcation sphere at $r_+$ into a second exterior, with the edge of the ergosphere marked as the class `ergo`; Kerr's throat is checked to have $\rho = 2GM/c^2$ at any spin and Kerr-Newman's $2GM/c^2 - r_Q^2/r_+$.
Schwarzschild-de Sitter's static slice at $t = 0$, drawn at $\Lambda = 0.2/r_s^2$, runs from the throat $r_h = 1.085\,r_s$ to the widest circle $r_c = 3.215\,r_s$, standing vertical at both, and on through the cosmological bifurcation sphere into the next static region, the same surface turned over, up to the next throat: one period of a chain without end, whose horizons are the exact roots of the cubic in the published $g_{rr}$, each coefficient of the expansion about a root reduced modulo the root's minimal polynomial before it is evaluated.
The charged black hole in de Sitter space, drawn as the lukewarm hole of its other diagrams, has three views: the static slice $t = 0$ from the throat $r_+ = 2r_s/3$ to the widest circle $r_c = 2\,r_s$ and on into the next static region, one period of a chain as Kottler's is; the static slice inside $r_-$, through the inner bifurcation sphere, each side running in until $g_{rr} = 1$ at the root $r = 0.2495\,r_s$ of $9r^4 + 64r_s^3r - 16r_s^4$, as Reissner and Nordström's does at $r_q^2/r_s$; and the moment $\tau = 1/H$ of the cosmological chart, whose circles have radius $\rho + r_s/2$, the infinitely long throat $z = w + \tfrac{1}{2}\ln((w - 1)/(w + 1))$ with $w = \sqrt{4\rho/r_s + 1}$ in units of $r_s$, the same surface over the areal radius at every $\tau > 0$, drawn from $\rho = r_s/100$; between $r_-$ and $r_+$, beyond $r_c$ and nearer the singularity than $0.2495\,r_s$ no surface carries the static slice, which the views state and the script checks.
The charged black hole in anti-de Sitter space, at $r_s = 27L/8$ and $r_q^2 = 11L^2/8$, has two views of its static slice $t = 0$: outside $r_+ = L$ through the throat into the second exterior, in flat space out to $1.3275\,L$ and in Minkowski space beyond, as Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter's is; and inside $r_- = L/2$ through the inner bifurcation sphere, in flat space in to $0.4163\,L$ and in Minkowski space nearer the singularity, where $g_{rr} < 1$ again and the sheet closes on $r = 0$ along a light cone, taken through radii in the ratio $1.02$ from $L/100$ and written to twelve decimals; the two level circles are the real roots of $8r^4 - 27r + 11$.
Boulware and Deser's black hole has two views: its static slice $t = 0$ at $r_0 = 13r_h/12$ and $\ell = 5r_h/12$, from the throat $r_h$ to $6\,r_h$ on both exteriors, checked against the quadrature of $dz/dr = \sqrt{2r_0^2/(r^2 + W - 2r_0^2)}$; and the other branch at $r_0 = \ell$ in Minkowski space, a sheet that leaves the centre along a cone of slope $\sqrt{1/2}$ and nears a light cone far out.
De Sitter's static slice at $t = 0$ is the sphere of radius $\ell = \sqrt{3/\Lambda}$, drawn at $\Lambda = 3$: the static chart covers the hemisphere out to the horizon and the antipodal observer's patch the other, the waist of the hyperboloid; the flat slicing's slices are flat, which the view states and the script checks.
Penrose's impulsive wave is one view, a movie of the plane across the string through the break from $ct = \ell/4$ to $5\ell/2$ at $\beta = 1/2$, a frame every $\ell/16$: behind the wave the moment $t$, a flat disc of radius $ct$, which is checked, and ahead of it the moment $T = t/\beta$ of the string's rest frame, the cone $\rho = \beta R$, the two meeting on the front in one circle; a ring of free particles rests on $R = 2\,\ell$ until the front reaches it and then falls at $3c/5$, and the map between the two frames is checked against the published retarded chart.
Israel's shell of dust is one view, a movie of the equator from $v - r = -5\,r_s$ to $r_s$, a frame every $r_s/16$: outside the shell Vaidya's slices of constant $v - r$, $z = 2\sqrt{r_sr}$, and inside it the moment of the flat time $T$ at which the slice meets the shell, a flat disc, which is checked; each frame is checked against that form, the two sides to meet in one circle, and the fold at the shell against $\sqrt{r_s/R}$.
The charged shell of dust has two views. The falling shell is a movie of the equator from $v - r = -1.5\,r_s$ to $r_s$, a frame every $r_s/32$: outside the shell the slices of constant $v - r$, on which the published metric pulls back to $(1 + r_s/r - r_q^2/r^2)\,dr^2 + r^2d\phi^2$ and $z = 2s - 2r_q\arctan(s/r_q)$ with $s = \sqrt{r_sr - r_q^2}$, and inside it the moment of the flat time $T$ at which the slice meets the shell, a flat disc, which is checked; each frame is checked against that form, the two sides to meet in one circle, and the fold at the shell against $\sqrt{r_sR - r_q^2}/R$. The balanced shell at rest, at $\epsilon = a$, is the slice $t = 0$ of the isotropic chart, $z = 2s + \ln((s - 1)/(s + 1))$ with $s = \sqrt{2r/a - 1}$, cut off by a flat disc, with the throat below the shell drawn on as a reference down to $\rho = a/64$; each view marks the shell's circle a thousandth of its unit inside the fold, where the page's turning geometry and the script agree on what the wall in front hides.
Vaidya is its imploding shell of radiation at four moments, played as a movie with a frame every $r_s/16$ of $v - r$ and the rim $r = 4\,r_s$ at $z = 0$ in every frame, slices of constant $v - r$, since a slice of constant $v$ is null; `Slice` pulls the published metric back along $v = T + r$ to $(1 + 2Gm/c^2r)\,dr^2 + r^2d\phi^2$, a flat disc inside the shell, which is checked, and $z^2 = 4r_sr$ outside, Flamm's paraboloid moved in by $r_s$, meeting at a `crease`.
Sultana and Dyer's black hole is a movie of the moments $\eta = 3$ to $6\,r_s$ of its Kerr-Schild chart at $\eta_0 = 3\,r_s$, a frame every $r_s/10$, each from the spike at the singularity $r = 0$ out to $r = 4\,r_s$: the paraboloid $z^2 = 4ar_s\rho$ over the areal radius $\rho = ar$, every frame the first magnified by $a = \eta^2/\eta_0^2$, checked against $\rho = ar$ and $z = 2a\sqrt{r_sr}$, with the event horizon and both trapping horizons among its circles.
Oppenheimer-Snyder is its dust released from rest at $R_0 = 2\,r_s$ at four moments of the dust's proper time, played as a movie with a frame about every $0.07\,r_s$ of $c\tau$ and the clocks released at $4\,r_s$, the rim of the drawing, at $z = 0$ in every frame: inside, the published interior chart at $a = (a_m/2)(1 + \cos\eta)$, checked to make the published $G^\chi{}_\chi$ vanish, a cap of a sphere of radius $a$; outside, the moment carried on as Novikov's slice, clocks released from rest at every radius with the dust, read from Tolman-Bondi's comoving chart with no dust in it, since a slice of constant Schwarzschild $t$ meets the dust at an angle after the release and cannot reach it inside $r_s$. `RestCloud` solves each shell's cycloid and is checked to make Tolman-Bondi's published $G^r{}_r$ vanish and its $G^t{}_t$ give the declared density; the two sides meet with one tangent, and at the release the outside is Flamm's paraboloid.
Tolman-Bondi is the cloud its spacetime diagram declares, density falling as $1 - r^2/r_b^2$ with $2GM/c^2 = r_b/2$, but released from rest, $E = -GM(r)/c^2r$, at four moments before its centre is crushed, played as a movie with a frame every $0.025\,r_b$ of $ct$ and the clocks released at $2.5\,r_b$, the rim of the drawing, at $z = 0$ in every frame, from its own comoving chart with `RestCloud`, checked against its published field equations; the spacetime diagram's cloud is marginally bound, $E = 0$, and its slices are planes, which the view states and the script checks.
Szekeres's cloud is the one its spacetime diagrams declare, at the moments $ct = -0.4$, $0$, $0.3$ and $0.55\,r_b$ of the axisymmetric chart, played as a movie with a frame every $0.025\,r_b$ and the plane outside the cloud at $z = 0$ in every frame: the surface $\theta = \pi/2$ through the equators of its shells, on which $g_{rr} = (\partial_rR)^2 + R^2S'^2/S^2$, so it climbs at $dz/dr = R\,S'/S$, the rate at which the centres of the shells move along the axis in the flat space of the moment, which the script checks by pulling Euclid's metric back onto the published one; each circle is checked against $\rho = R$ and the height of its shell's centre, at $ct = 0$ against $z = 2r^3/3 - 2r^5/5 - 4/15$, the cloud against the published $G^r{}_r = 0$ and its density, and every height is drawn three times over, since the dish is $4/15$ deep and $2.5$ wide.
Every surface of the cloud is checked at the height the metric gives it and then drawn twice as tall, `vertical` 2, since at its own height the cloud rises by only a third of its width and reads on the page as a flat disc, as the captain found on 30 September 2026.
Bertotti-Robinson, a product of two dimensional anti-de Sitter space and a sphere of the one radius $b$, has two views: its equator at one moment, a line of the first factor times a great circle of the second, the cylinder $\rho = b$, $z = b\ln r$, and its sphere of $\theta$ and $\phi$ at one event, the second factor; the first factor is Lorentzian and has no surface, which the view states.
Van Stockum's rotating dust, at $R = 1$ as its spacetime diagrams declare, is the plane $z = 0$ at one moment, whose circles grow to $r = R/\sqrt{2}$ and shrink after, so the surface curls back toward the axis until $g_{rr} = (d\rho/dr)^2$ at $r = 0.83\,R$, found by sympy; the view states that nothing is drawn beyond, and that beyond $r = R$ the circles are closed timelike curves, both checked.
Som and Raychaudhuri's universe, in units of $r_c = c/\Omega$, is the plane $z = 0$ about one world line of the dust at one moment: $g_{rr} = 1$ and the circles have radius $r\sqrt{1 - r^2/r_c^2}$, widest at $r_c/\sqrt{2}$, and $g_{rr} = (d\rho/dr)^2$ at $r = \sqrt{3}\,r_c/2$ exactly, where the surface stops; it is checked against the closed form of its height, and the view states that beyond $r_c$ the circles are closed timelike curves.
Bonnor's rotating dust cloud, at $a = 1$, is its plane $z = 0$ at one moment outside the null circle, from $\rho = 1.0002\,a$ to $5\,a$: with $x = a^4/\rho^4$ the circles have radius $\rho\sqrt{1 - x}$ and $(dR/d\rho)^2 = (1 + x)^2/(1 - x)$ exceeds $g_{\rho\rho} = e^{x/8}$ everywhere, which is checked, so the whole sheet is drawn in Minkowski space, leaving the axis along the light cone and flattening far out, where $(dR/d\rho)^2 - g_{\rho\rho} \to 23a^4/8\rho^4$; the view states that inside $\rho = a$ the circles are closed timelike curves, and the spherical chart's equator is checked to give the same surface.
Taub-NUT, at $m = 1$ and $l = m/2$ as its spacetime diagram declares, is the equator of a slice of constant $t$, where $g_{t\phi} \propto \cos\theta$ vanishes, out of its horizon $r_+ = m + \sqrt{m^2 + l^2}$, whose circumference radius $\sqrt{2(mr_+ + l^2)}$ is checked; across the horizon lies Taub's cosmology, where $r$ is a time, which the view states and the script checks.
Godel's universe, at $\omega = 1$ as its spacetime diagrams declare, is the plane $z = 0$ about one world line of the dust at one moment of the cylindrical chart, which stops exactly where $\sinh^2 r = 1/\sqrt{2}$, since $g_{rr} = (d\rho/dr)^2$ there reduces to $4s^3 = 2s$; the view states that beyond it no surface carries the slice and that beyond $\sinh r = 1$ the circles are closed timelike curves, both checked.
Van Den Broeck's drive is the pocket its spacetime diagrams declare, the equator of a moment of $t$ inside the bubble in Krasnikov's chart, from the centre of the pocket at $l = -4R$ out to the circle $r = R$ where the wall begins: a flat floor, a rim that climbs $\pi R/4$, a flat lid, a neck that climbs $\pi R/4$ more, and the flat plane outside, checked against $z = (w\sqrt{1 - w^2} + \arcsin w)/4 + \pi/8$ on the rim and the neck, $w$ twice the distance from the middle of each, and against $\rho = r(l)$; the comoving spherical chart, with the $B$ the same pocket has, is checked to draw the same circles at the same heights and distances. Van Den Broeck's own polynomial for $B$ has circles that shrink faster than the distance in to them, since a surface in flat space needs $-2 \le r\,B'/B \le 0$ and so $1 + \alpha \le (1 + \tilde\Delta/\tilde R)^2$, which `van_den_broeck.md` derives.
Tippett and Tsang's bubble draws the turn of the light cone as a height, as the Krasnikov tube does: $\chi = \tfrac12\arctan(g_{tx}/g_{xx})$ from the published metric, the angle of the axis the two null directions of the plane of $t$ and $x$ stand $45°$ either side of, over the plane $z = 0$ at $ct = A/2$, where the two boxes stand about $x = \pm\sqrt{3}A/2$ and are turned by $\pm 30°$; that moment is a moment of space everywhere, which is checked on $g_{xx}$, the null directions are checked against the published metric at four thousand points, and the curves where the turn is $15°$ are marked.
Ellis-Bronnikov is the same catenoid in its own chart, whose $r$ is the proper distance from the throat, so it is one piece through the throat, checked against $z = \ell\,\mathrm{arcsinh}(r/\ell)$ and $\rho = \sqrt{r^2 + \ell^2}$.
The cosmic string is its cone at $4G\mu/c^2 = 0.1$ with Gott's core rounding the apex, and the figure lays the cone flat beside it, cut along one meridian, so that the missing wedge $\delta = 36°$ shows.
Its first view is a movie of the ideal string's cone unrolling onto the plane, as the captain asked on 30 September 2026, cut along $\phi = 0$: in each frame the cone of half angle $\alpha$ with $\sin\alpha = 2\pi(1 - 4G\mu/c^2)/(2\pi - \Delta\phi)$ carries the whole of it round $2\pi - \Delta\phi$ of the axis, so the cut's edges stand $\Delta\phi$ apart, from $0$, the cone, to the deficit angle $8\pi G\mu/c^2 = 36°$, where it lies flat.
Every distance from the apex and every circle keeps its length in every frame, which the script checks, as it checks the wedge of the last.
Gott's core is a cap of a sphere and does not unroll without stretching, so it stays on the second view.
The Einstein static universe is the equator of one moment of its hyperspherical chart, the sphere $\rho = R\sin\chi$, $z = -R\cos\chi$ from the pole to the antipode, the same at every moment, with the equator where the areal chart and Einstein's projection end marked as `chartedge`; the areal chart's slice is checked to be its lower hemisphere.
Its conformal diagram is the strip $0 \le \chi \le \pi$ of $\eta = ct/R$, and three views draw Minkowski, de Sitter and anti-de Sitter space inside it by the maps their own diagrams use, each checked to make the pulled back metric of the Einstein static universe one conformal factor times the other's, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ and on its spheres; `_tools/derivations/einstein_static.md` is the derivation.
The C-metric, at $\alpha m = 1/6$ and $C = 1/(1 + 2\alpha m) = 3/4$ as its other diagrams declare, has two views: the equator of $t = 0$, $dr^2/Q + C^2r^2\,d\phi^2$, from one black hole's horizon at $r = 2m$ out to the acceleration horizon at $r = 1/\alpha$, its widest circle, and on through that horizon's bifurcation into the second black hole's exterior, $\rho = Cr$ checked; and the black hole horizon itself, smooth at $\theta = 0$ and the apex of a cone at $\theta = \pi$, where the string meets it, with $d\rho/ds$ checked to be $C(1 \pm 2\alpha m)$ at its poles and its area against Griffiths, Krtouš and Podolský's $16\pi Cm^2/(1 - 4\alpha^2m^2)$.
The Milne universe is the equator of a moment of its comoving hyperbolic chart at $ct = 0.5$, $1$, $2$ and $3$ in any length $\ell$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.05$ of $ct$: its circles grow faster than the distance out to them at every $\chi > 0$, which is checked, so each moment is drawn in Minkowski space as the sheet $Z = ct\cosh\chi$, $\rho = ct\sinh\chi$ out to $\rho = 4\,\ell$, inside the light cone $Z = \rho$ drawn as a reference.
The Coleman-De Luccia bubble is the equator of a moment of the open universe inside a bubble whose new vacuum has negative energy, $a = \ell\sin(c\tau/\ell)$, at $c\tau = \pi\ell/6$ to $5\pi\ell/6$ in steps of $\pi\ell/6$, played as a movie with a frame about every $0.05\,\ell$ of $c\tau$: the hyperbolic plane of radius $a$, drawn in Minkowski space as Milne's is and checked against $\rho = a\sinh\chi$ and $Z = a\cosh\chi$, with the scale factor checked to make the open chart's published Einstein tensor $3g_{\mu\nu}/\ell^2$; its moments lie on the drawings of that bubble's inside and on no other, which `HIDDEN` records.
That Minkowski space is the plane $\theta = \pi/2$ of its own inertial chart, $Z = cT$, so every sheet stands where its moment lies; `_tools/derivations/milne.md` is the derivation.
The domain wall is the equator of the global chart at $kct = -1$, $-0.5$, $0$, $0.5$ and $1$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.05$ of $kct$, from the centre of one side through the wall to the centre of the other: its circles grow at $\cosh(kct)$ times the distance out to them, which is checked, so each moment is drawn in Minkowski space as two cones $Z = z\sinh(kct)$ joined rim to rim at the wall, and at $ct = 0$ as the flat disc of radius $1/k$ taken twice; `_tools/derivations/domain_wall.md` is the derivation.
Randall and Sundrum's wall is the surface of $y$ and $x_1$ at one moment, a strip of width $2\pi/k$ along $x_1$ rolled up so that $x_1$ takes the angle's place in `Slice`: the two halves of Beltrami's pseudosphere joined at its rim, the wall, a `crease`, each checked against the tractrix $kZ = \mathrm{arcosh}\,e^{k|y|} - \sqrt{1 - e^{-2k|y|}}$ out to $|y| = 3/k$; its second view is the same surface between two walls at $kr_c = 1/2$, cut at $\phi = \pm\pi$, both copies drawn.
Lifshitz spacetime is the surface of $\rho$ and $x$ at one moment of its proper distance chart, a strip of width $2\pi L$ along $x$ rolled up as Randall and Sundrum's is, the same for every $z$, which is checked at $z = 2$ and $3$: half of Beltrami's pseudosphere below $\rho = 0$, checked against the tractrix down to $\rho = -3L$, toward the singularity, and above it, where the circles grow faster than the distance out to them, a sheet in Minkowski space checked against $Z = \sqrt{e^{2\rho/L} - 1} - \arctan\sqrt{e^{2\rho/L} - 1}$ out to $r = 3L$, the two meeting level at $r = L$ with one tangent.
Wahlquist's fluid is its equatorial plane at one moment of its own time, the body the spacetime diagrams draw: the disc $\xi = 0$ from the centre to the ring and the plane $\eta = 0$ from the ring to the surface of zero pressure, joined on the ring with one tangent, each slice read in the form `wahlquist_stable` writes, in which the ring's $0/0$ cancels, and each circle checked against Wahlquist's formulas in forty digits; a second view is Whittaker's sphere with the same $b$, checked against $R_0\sin X$ and $dz/dX = R_0\sqrt{1/F - \cos^2X}$.
The Einstein-Rosen waves are the pulse of Weber, Wheeler, and Bonnor at $C = a$ going out from the axis, the plane $z = 0$ of the cylindrical chart out to $\rho = 10\,a$ at the moments $ct = a$, $2a$, $4a$ and $8a$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.1\,a$ of $ct$ and the axis at $z = 0$ in every frame, with $\psi$ and $\gamma$ entering the slice as numbers and the pulse checked to make every published Einstein component vanish; each circle's radius is checked against $\rho e^{-\psi}$, and at $t = 0$ no surface of revolution in flat space carries the moment out to $\rho = 1.72\,a$, which the view states and the script checks.
The Nariai universe, at $\Lambda = 1$, has two views: the circle of $\chi$ times a great circle of the sphere at five moments of its global chart, from $ct = -1.5$ to $1.5$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.1$ of $ct$, each the cylinder $\rho = \cosh(ct)$ of height $2\pi$ whose top and bottom edges are one circle, $\rho$ and $z = \theta$ checked; and the sphere of $\theta$ and $\phi$ at one event, as Bertotti-Robinson's.
The global monopole, at $\Delta = 0.19$, has two views: the monopole with no mass at its centre, the equator of the Barriola-Vilenkin chart at $t = 0$, a cone of half angle $\arcsin 0.9$ out to $r = 3\ell$ whose apex is the singular centre, $\rho = 0.9r$ and $z = \sqrt{0.19}\,r$ checked; and Letelier's black hole at $r_s = 1$, the equator of the static chart at $t = 0$ through the throat $r_h = 100/81$ into a second exterior out to $6\,r_s$, checked against its closed form $z = (w\sqrt{\Delta w^2 + 1} + \mathrm{arsinh}(\sqrt{\Delta}\,w)/\sqrt{\Delta})/(1 - \Delta)^{3/2}$ with $w = \sqrt{(1 - \Delta)r/r_s - 1}$, which opens far out into the monopole's cone.
The dilaton black hole, at $r_d = r_s/2$, has three views, the equator of the moment $t = 0$ in the Einstein metric and in each string metric, each through the bifurcation sphere $r = r_s$ into a second exterior out to $6\,r_s$: the Einstein metric's throat has the radius $\sqrt{r_s(r_s - r_d)}$, the magnetic string metric's the radius $r_s$, and the electric string metric's surface is Flamm's paraboloid $z = 2\sqrt{(r_s - r_d)(r - r_s)}$ with $\rho = r - r_d$, checked; the Einstein charts' drawings mark the Einstein metric's moment and each string chart's its own, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
Tangherlini's black hole has two views at $r_h = 1$, the plane of $r$ and $\phi$ at $t = 0$ with every other angle at $\pi/2$, through the throat into a second exterior out to $6\,r_h$: in five dimensions the catenoid $z = r_h\,\mathrm{arcosh}(r/r_h)$, and in six the surface $z = z_\infty - 2\sqrt{r_h^3/r}\,{}_2F_1(\tfrac{1}{2}, \tfrac{1}{6}; \tfrac{7}{6}; r_h^3/r^3)$, which approaches the height $z_\infty = \tfrac{1}{3}B(\tfrac{1}{6}, \tfrac{1}{2})\,r_h = 2.4286\,r_h$, each checked against its closed form and against $\rho = r$; five dimensions and six are two spacetimes, so each view is marked on the drawings of its own charts alone, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
The Kaluza-Klein monopole is the surface of $r$ and $x_5$ on the half axis $\theta = 0$ at one moment, at $m = 1$, from the nut out to $r = 16m$: `Slice` sweeps $x_5 = 8m\phi$, since the fifth dimension's period is $16\pi m$, and the surface is a cigar whose circles have radius $8m\sqrt{r/(r + 4m)}$, smooth at the nut, $d\rho/ds = 1$ checked there, and a cylinder of radius $8m$ far away, its height checked against an independent quadrature.
The Eguchi-Hanson space is three totally geodesic surfaces of Eguchi and Hanson's chart at $a = 1$, each a view, since the space has no moments: the fibre, the surface of $r$ and $\psi$ over the pole $\theta = 0$ of the bolt, whose circle at $r$ has radius $(r/2)\sqrt{1 - a^4/r^4}$ and which rises at $dz/dr = \sqrt{3 + a^4/r^4}/2$, smooth on the bolt, where the radius grows as fast as the distance, and a cone of half angle $30°$ far away, whose circles are half a plane's because $\psi$ has the period $2\pi$; the bolt $r = a$, the round sphere of radius $a/2$ that $\theta$ and $\phi$ cover; and the surface $\theta = \pi/2$ at $\psi = 0$ and $\psi = \pi$, the fixed set of $\theta \to \pi - \theta$, $\psi \to -\psi$, two halves of radius $r/2$ that meet on the bolt's equator at $dz/dr = \pm\sqrt{(3r^4 + a^4)/(4(r^4 - a^4))}$ and both run out to the one boundary. Each radius and each height is checked against its closed form or its quadrature, and `_tools/test_eguchi_hanson.py` holds the file to them; `eguchi_hanson.md` Step 5 is the derivation.
The gravitational instantons are nine such surfaces, each a view of its own chart, since a chart there is a space: the cigar of the Euclidean Schwarzschild solution, the surface of $r$ and $\tau$ with $\tau = 2r_s\phi$, whose circle has radius $2r_s\sqrt{1 - r_s/r}$, smooth on the bolt and a cylinder of radius $2r_s$ far away, drawn again in the regular chart, where its radius is $x$; Flamm's paraboloid through the bolt's equator, the surface $\theta = \pi/2$ at $\tau = 0$ and $\tau = 2\pi r_s$; the nut's surface of the self-dual Taub-NUT chart and the fibre over a point of Taub-bolt's bolt, each read at $\theta = 0$ with $\tau = 2n\phi$, which takes $\tau + 2n\phi$ once round $8\pi n$; the axis of two centres at $z = \pm 2n$ with $\epsilon = 1$, three pieces that touch at the centres, a sphere between them and a cigar beyond each, read from the free chart with $V$ and $\omega$ given on each part of the axis; a complex line of $\mathbb{CP}^2$, the round sphere of radius $L/2$, read from the distance chart for both of its charts, since the chart in $r$ reaches its far pole only at infinity; and the fibre of Page's space from one bolt to the other at $\Lambda = 1$ and the root $\nu$ of his quartic, `PAGE_NU`, to thirty digits. `_tools/derivations/gravitational_instantons.md` Step 9 is the working, and the script took 177 seconds for the nine on 2 October 2026.
The Kaluza-Klein black holes have three views at $t = 0$, each through the horizon into the other exterior: the equator in the Einstein metric of four dimensions at $q = 2\,r_s$, whose throat has the radius $(q\,r_s^3)^{1/4}$, and the surface of $r$ and $y$ at $\theta = 0$ of the electric hole, swept at $y = r_s\phi$ for a circle of length $2\pi r_s$, and of the magnetic hole at $p = 2\,r_s$, swept at $y = 2\sqrt{p(p - r_s)}\,\phi$; the circle's radius is $r_s\sqrt{1 + (q - r_s)/r}$, widest on the horizon, and $2\sqrt{p(p - r_s)}\sqrt{r/(r + p - r_s)}$, narrowest there, and each height is checked against its quadrature.
Myers and Perry's black hole has four views, each at one moment of $t$ through the throat into a second exterior: with one spin in five dimensions at $\mu = 1$ and $a = 3/5$, the plane of rotation, whose circles have the radius $\sqrt{r^2 + a^2 + \mu a^2/r^2}$, $\mu/r_+ = 5/4$ at the throat, with the edge of the ergosphere marked, and the transverse plane, the stretched catenoid $z = \sqrt{\mu}\,\mathrm{arcosh}(r/r_+)$ with a throat of radius $r_+ = 4/5$; with equal spins at $a = 2/5$, the surface of $\rho$ and the Hopf fibre at $\theta = \pi$, swept by $\psi = 3\phi$, whose circles have the radius $\sqrt{\rho^4 + \mu a^2}/\rho$; and in six dimensions at $a = 3/2$ the transverse plane, of finite height. Each height is checked against an independent quadrature, and each drawing marks the view it draws, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
Black Saturn has one view, the plane of its ring, $\rho = 0$, at one moment of $t$, for the Saturn the spacetime diagrams draw: the surface of $z$ and $\psi$ with circles of radius $Le^V$, as far as flat space carries it. Near the ring the rotation has widened the circles, which shrink inward faster than the distance in to them, so the plane is drawn from far out in to $z = 0.242$ and the band about the hole from $z = 0.787$ in to the circle of radius $7L/10$ where the slice crosses the hole's horizon, each lying level where it stops, with those two circles set at one height; `ck.stops` holds $g_{zz} - (d\rho/dz)^2$ negative between them and the ring's edges, whose radii $\sqrt{15/2}\,L$ and $\sqrt{147/40}\,L$ are checked. The ring alone has no view: at $x = \pm 1$ its chart's $g_{xx}$ is not finite, and the pullback along the swept angle cannot be read there.
Neugebauer and Meinel's disc has one view, the plane of the disc at one moment of $t$ for the disc of $\mu = 3$, circles of radius $\rho\,e^{-\nu}$ read from Bardeen and Wagoner's chart with the tables of `nm_disc.py`: the circles reach $4.41\,\rho_0$ at $0.92\,\rho_0$, narrow to $4.07\,\rho_0$ at $1.20\,\rho_0$ and widen again, the beginning of the throat, and where they change faster than the distance across them, inside $0.689\,\rho_0$ and between $0.979$ and $1.006\,\rho_0$, the slice stands in Minkowski space, four pieces joined on three level circles. The slopes are read as numbers, `sl.numeric`, since sympy takes no limit of a table, and the knots of the band about the rim crowd toward the rim from outside, where the slope changes as a square root.
Damour and Solodukhin's wormhole is Flamm's paraboloid on both sides of the throat $r = r_s$ out to $6\,r_s$, the same surface at every $\lambda$ since its $g_{rr}$ is Schwarzschild's, checked against $z = 2\sqrt{r_s(r - r_s)}$ in its own chart and the isotropic one and against $z = 2\sqrt{r_s}\,u$ in Einstein and Rosen's.
The wormhole of Einstein-Dirac-Maxwell theory is its equator at one moment on both sides of the throat $r = r_0$ out to $6\,r_0$, at $r_0 = 1$ and $Q_e = 1/2$, its height checked in all three charts against an independent quadrature in Bronnikov and Kim's $u$, $dz/du = 2\sqrt{(r_0^2 + (r_0 + b)u^2)/(r_0 - b + u^2)}$ with $b = Q_e^2/r_0$; far out it approaches Flamm's paraboloid with $r_s = r_0 + b$.
Kiselev's black hole has two views, each the equator of the static moment $t = 0$ at $w = -2/3$, read from the chart of the linear term and belonging to every chart. With the black hole, at $r_q = 8\,r_s$, the surface runs from the throat $r_- = 1.172\,r_s$ to the widest circle $r_+ = 6.828\,r_s$ and on into the next static region, one period of a chain as Kottler's is. Without it, at $r_q = 1$, $dz/dr = \sqrt{r/(r_q - r)}$ and the profile is the cycloid of a circle of diameter $r_q$, $r = \tfrac{1}{2}r_q(1 - \cos\psi)$ and $z = \tfrac{1}{2}r_q(\psi - \sin\psi)$, from the centre, a curvature singularity, to the horizon $r = r_q$ and on into the second static region, a closed surface, which is checked against that form.
Einstein and Rosen's bridges have three views, each the equator of a moment of $t$: the neutral bridge, Flamm's paraboloid on both sheets out to $6\,r_s$, checked against $z = 2\sqrt{r_s}\,u$ in their chart and in the isotropic one; the charged bridge with no mass, the catenoid $z = r_q\,\mathrm{arsinh}(u/r_q)$ out to $|u| = 5\,r_q$, checked in the areal radius at $r_s = 0$ as $z = r_q\,\mathrm{arcosh}(r/r_q)$; and the charged bridge with a mass at $r_q = \sqrt{3}\,r_s/2$, both sheets from $r_+ = 3r_s/2$ out to $6\,r_s$ by quadrature. They are three spacetimes of one page, so each drawing marks the moment of its own, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
Simpson and Visser's black bounce has four views at $r_s = 1$. The black bounce $a = r_s/2$ outside its horizon is the equator of $t = 0$ read in the areal chart, where $g_{\rho\rho}$ is rational and the horizon is its root $\rho = r_s$, on both sheets through the bifurcation sphere out to $6\,r_s$, checked steeper than Flamm's paraboloid at every radius and against scipy's quadrature of $\sqrt{g_{\rho\rho} - 1}$. Between its horizons $r$ is the time, and a moment of constant $r$ is a flat cylinder of radius $\rho = \sqrt{r^2 + a^2}$ on which the stretch $|ct| \le r_s$ is $2r_s\sqrt{r_s/\rho - 1}$ long, five moments from $r = 0.75\,r_s$ to $-0.75\,r_s$ played as a movie with a frame every $0.05\,r_s$ of $r$ whose `value` is the proper time since the horizon, so that the cylinder narrows to the radius $a$ and widens again at a steady proper time. The one way wormhole $a = r_s$ is an infinitely long throat closing on the radius $r_s$, drawn from $r = r_s/50$ to $6\,r_s$, and the traversable wormhole $a = 2\,r_s$ is one piece through the throat from $-6\,r_s$ to $6\,r_s$, each checked against $\rho = \sqrt{r^2 + a^2}$ and the quadrature of its height, the last against the catenoid's slope $a/\rho$ at $r_s = 0$. The three are three spacetimes of one line element, so each drawing marks its own moments, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records, with `HIDDEN_SURFACES` for the areal chart, drawn on the side $r > 0$.
Fisher, Janis, Newman and Winicour's scalar field is its equator at one moment at $\gamma = 1/2$ and $b = 1$, read in the harmonic chart, where $w = e^{-u/4}$ and nothing cancels toward the singularity: in Minkowski space from a circle of radius $0.0126\,b$ beside the singular point, which the surface enters along the light cone, out to $r = 9b/8$, where it lies level, and in flat space beyond, out to $4b$, each circle checked against $w/(1 - w^4)$ and each height against the quadrature of $\sqrt{|9w^4 - 1|}/(1 - w^4)^{3/2}$; the near piece is written to fourteen decimals, since $d\rho$ and $dZ$ agree there to $8f$, and the spherical, isotropic and Janis-Newman-Winicour charts are checked to give the same surface.
Witten's black hole in two dimensions is its Euclidean section, his cigar, at $\lambda = 1$, since a moment of a spacetime of two dimensions is a line: `Slice` sweeps the time of Witten's chart through the imaginary direction, $t = -i\phi$, which makes $g_{\phi\phi} = -g_{tt} = \tanh^2 r$, and the surface of $r$ and $\theta = i\lambda ct$ is drawn from the horizon $r = 0$ out to $\lambda r = 3$, each circle checked against $\tanh r$, the height against $\mathrm{arsinh}(\cosh r) - \sqrt{1 + 1/\cosh^2 r} + \sqrt{2} - \mathrm{arsinh} 1$, and the tip against $d\rho/ds = 1$, which is what fixes the period $2\pi$ of $\theta$ and with it the temperature; its meridians $\theta = 0$ and $\pi$ are the moment $t = 0$ on the two sides of the horizon, which is the slice the other diagrams mark.
The black hole of Jackiw and Teitelboim's gravity is its Euclidean section, at $L = r_h = 1$: `Slice` sweeps the time of the proper distance chart through the imaginary direction, which makes the surface of $\rho$ and $\theta = ir_hct/L^2$ the hyperbolic plane $d\rho^2 + L^2\sinh^2(\rho/L)\,d\theta^2$, whose circles grow faster than the distance out to them, which is checked, so it is drawn in Minkowski space as the sheet $Z = \cosh\rho - 1$ of a hyperboloid out to $\rho = 2L$ with its light cone, each circle checked against $\sinh\rho$ and the tip against $d\rho_{\text{circle}}/ds = 1$, which fixes the period of $\theta$ and the temperature; its meridians are the moment $t = 0$ on the two sides of the horizon, which the other diagrams mark.
Roberts's collapsing scalar field has three views, one for each outcome, each a movie of the equator of a moment of Roberts's $t$ from $ct = -1.6\,\ell$ to $1.6\,\ell$, a frame every $\ell/10$, read in the diagonal chart out to $\rho = 5\,\ell$.
Every moment but $t = 0$ stands in three dimensional Minkowski space, since $(dR/d\rho)^2 - 1 = p^2c^2t^2/4R^2$, with its rim at $Z = 0$: it begins on the first ray $\rho = -ct$ before $t = 0$, and after it on the last ray $\rho = ct$ for $p < 1$ and at the singularity $\rho = pct$ for $p \ge 1$, which it enters along the light cone.
Each frame is checked against $R = \sqrt{\rho(\rho - pct)}$ and $Z = pct\ln(\sqrt{\rho} + \sqrt{\rho - pct})$, and the profile toward the singular point starts $2 \times 10^{-4}$ of $pct$ short of it, steps by a fiftieth of $R$, and is written to fourteen decimals.
The three are three spacetimes of one line element, so each drawing marks its own moments, $ct = \pm 0.8\,\ell$ and $\pm 1.6\,\ell$, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
The gravastar is its equator at one moment of $t$, at $R = 5r_s/4$ and $L = 2\,r_s$: a cap of the sphere of radius $L$ inside the shell, checked against $z = L - \sqrt{L^2 - r^2}$, and Flamm's paraboloid outside it out to $4\,r_s$, with the vacuum paraboloid drawn on under the cap as a reference; the two meet in one circle at a crease, the cap reaching the shell at $dz/dr = 0.80$ and the paraboloid leaving it at $2$, both checked, since $g_{rr}$ jumps there by the shell's surface density.
Ehlers and Kundt's B I is one view, a movie of the surface of $r$ and $\phi$ on the de Sitter slicing from $\tau = -0.8$ to $0.8$, a frame every $0.05$, at $b = 1$ and $z = 0$: circles of radius $r\cosh\tau$ with $dz/dr = \sqrt{r/(r - b) - \cosh^2\tau}$, Flamm's paraboloid on both sheets at $\tau = 0$, which is checked, and away from it a neck of radius $b\cosh\tau$ and a surface that lies level at $r = b\cosh^2\tau/\sinh^2\tau$, beyond which the script checks that no surface of revolution carries the moment.
Bonnor and Vaidya's charged shell is one view, a movie of the equator on slices of constant $v - r$ from $-3\,M$ to $-q^2/2M$, a frame every $M/16$, flat inside the shell and $z = 2s - 2q\arctan(s/q)$ with $s = \sqrt{2Mr - q^2}$ outside it, each frame checked against that form and the fold at the shell against $\sqrt{2M/R - q^2/R^2}$.
The last frame has the shell on $r = q^2/2M$, where the surface lies level and the fold is gone, and its profile is written to more decimals, since it leaves that sphere as $(r - q^2/2M)^{3/2}$.
Mass inflation is the tail falling in alone, the input of its first spacetime diagram: slices of constant $v - r$ from $7.5$ to $16.5\,m_0$, a movie with a frame every $m_0/4$, each climbing at $dz/dr = \sqrt{2m/r - r_q^2/r^2}$ from the circle $r = r_q^2/2m$, where it lies level, out to $4\,m_0$, checked against that quadrature and written to the digits of a surface in Minkowski space, since its chords are short where it leaves the level circle; the apparent horizons are marked where $r^2 - 2mr + r_q^2$ vanishes at each circle's own advanced time. Its moments lie on the first spacetime diagram and on neither the view behind the shell nor the conformal diagram, which draw Ori's shell, as `HIDDEN` records.
Hiscock's evaporating black hole is a movie of the model its other diagrams declare, the slices $v - r = -5$ to $11\,m_0$, a frame every $m_0/4$ and the rim $r = 6\,m_0$ at $z = 0$ in every frame: inside the surface of pair creation `Slice` pulls the ingoing chart back along $v = T + r$, and outside it the outgoing chart along $u = T' - r$ with `null_rays.hiscock_outer_time`, both to $(1 + 2m/r)\,dr^2 + r^2d\phi^2$, and the two pieces are checked to meet on the surface with one mass. Each profile is checked against the quadrature of $\sqrt{2m/r}$ written out from the declared mass; a `Slice` may name a declared function of `null_rays.DECLARED_FUNCTIONS`.
The two are two spacetimes of one line element, so the static and Eddington-Finkelstein drawings mark the black hole's moment and the Barriola-Vilenkin drawings the monopole's, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
On its static plane each global moment is the curve $\sinh(ct) = \sinh(ct_k)/\sqrt{1 - r^2}$ from horizon to horizon, and `slices.py` checks that map by pulling the static metric back onto the global one.
Israel, Wilson and Perjés's fields have three views, one for each chart, each an equator at $t = 0$ in units of $m$: the source of complex mass at $l = m/2$ from $r = 1.02\,m$ to $6m$, an infinitely long throat checked against $\rho = \sqrt{r^2 + l^2}$; the spinning source at $a = m$ from $r = -0.21\,m$ to $6m$, which stops where $g_{rr} = (d\rho/dr)^2$, outside the circle $r = -0.60\,m$ where $g_{\phi\phi}$ vanishes, both found by the script and checked; and the midplane of Hartle and Hawking's two sources from $\rho = 1.56\,m$ to $8m$, checked against $\sqrt{W^2\rho^2 - \omega^2/W^2}$.
Majumdar and Papapetrou's spacetime has two views, each belonging to every chart: one hole's equator at $t = 0$ in the isotropic chart, $m = 1$, from $r = m/50$ to $4m$, an infinitely long throat whose circumference radius $r + m$ closes on $m$, checked against $\rho = r + m$ and $z = 2w + \ln((w - 1)/(w + 1))$ with $w = \sqrt{2r/m + 1}$; and the midplane $z = 0$ of two holes at $z = \pm 2m$ in the cylindrical chart, out to $\rho = 4m$, flat at the axis, checked against $\rho U$.
Einstein, Infeld and Hoffmann's field has one view, belonging to both charts: the plane $x = 0$ midway between the two equal bodies its spacetime diagrams declare, at $t = 0$, turned about the line through the bodies, out to $s = 30\,m$, flat at that line, checked against $\rho = s\sqrt{1 + 4m/\sqrt{s^2 + 100m^2}}$ and against the standard gauge's slice; at another moment the plane is no surface of revolution, so one moment is drawn.
Misner's and Brill and Lindquist's initial data are a moment already, so their three views are surfaces of the slice. The sphere through both holes, $\eta = \pi/2$ of Misner's bispherical chart, for two equal holes of $\alpha = 1$ at $z = \pm a$: its circle at $\mu$ has the radius $a\Psi^2$ with $\Psi = (\cosh\mu)^{-1/2} + \sqrt{2}\cosh(\mu/2)/a$, it flattens toward each pole into the plane of the sheet beyond that hole, and it is drawn in three pieces that join where each hole's throat cuts it, as a movie in $a$ from $1/2$ to $2$; the apparent horizon of the pair cuts it for $a$ from $1.4126$ to $1.5324$. The plane $z = 0$ between the holes in the cylindrical chart, a movie over the same $a$, with the circle where the apparent horizon cuts it marked for $a < 1.5324$. And one hole's equator in the isotropic chart, Flamm's paraboloid on both sheets as one piece through the throat $r = r_s/4$. `_tools/derivations/two_holes.py` finds the minimal surfaces by shooting curves from the axis, `_tools/test_misner_brill_lindquist.py` holds it to a single hole's throat, to Penrose's bound and to the critical separation $1.532$ of Alcubierre and others, and holds the file to its closed forms; `misner_brill_lindquist.md` Steps 5 and 6 are the derivation.
One hole alone is another spacetime than two, so each drawing marks only its own moment, which the `Slices` tests list in `HIDDEN_VIEWS`.
Brill's waves are a moment already too, and both their views are of the plane $z = 0$ of Holz, Miller, Wakano and Wheeler's wave, $q = a\rho^2e^{-r^2}$ in units of its width, with $\psi$ from `_tools/derivations/brill_wave.py`, which solves Brill's equation in Legendre polynomials and Chebyshev's points. For $a$ from $11$ to $13$ the plane is one piece and plays as a movie in $a$: a long narrow stalk where $e^q$ stretches the distance between circles, with the circle where the apparent horizon cuts it marked from $a = 11.82$ and a neck in its own circles from $12.19$. Below $a = 10.8$ the circles outgrow the distance out to them in a band, so the weaker wave, $a = 4$, is drawn in two pieces with the band left out, as Black Saturn's plane is. `_tools/test_brill_waves.py` holds the solver to Brill's positive mass integral, to the masses and the amplitude $11.82$ of Alcubierre and others and to Penrose's bound, and holds the file to its metric; `brill_waves.md` Steps 4 to 6 are the derivation.
The Curzon-Chazy particle is its plane $z = 0$ at one moment at $m = 1$, from $\rho = 0.7226\,m$, where $e^{-m^2/\rho^2} = (1 - m/\rho)^2$ and the surface lies level, through its neck of radius $m\,e$ at $\rho = m$ out to $5\,m$, each circle checked against $\rho\,e^{m/\rho}$; inside it no surface of revolution carries the slice, which the view states and the script checks, and the spherical chart's equator is checked to give the same surface.
Zipoy and Voorhees's metric has two views, its equatorial plane at one moment at $m = 1$ in the spherical chart: the oblate $q = 1$ from $r = 2.5161\,m$, where $r^2(r - 2m)^2 = (3m - r)(r - m)^3$ and the surface lies level, through its neck of radius $3\sqrt{3}\,m$ at $r = 3\,m$ out to $6\,m$, and the prolate $q = -1/2$ from $r = 2.0020\,m$, a circle of radius $0.356\,m$, out to $6\,m$, each circle checked against $r f^{-q/2}$; inside each no surface of revolution carries the slice, which the view states and the script checks, and the prolate spheroidal chart's equator is checked to give the same surfaces. Each drawing of an equatorial plane marks its own deformation's moment, which the `Slices` tests list in `HIDDEN_VIEWS`.
McVittie's mass is a movie of the moments $ct = 1$ to $7\,r_s$ of its isotropic chart, a frame every $0.2$, for the universe its other diagrams declare: every moment is Flamm's paraboloid over the areal radius $R = ar(1 + r_s/4ar)^2$, from the throat $R = r_s$, where the moment stops on the curvature singularity, out to $R = 6\,r_s$, so the surface is the same in every frame and what moves is the circles of constant comoving $r$ and, from $ct = 2.10\,r_s$, the two circles where $1 - r_s/R - H^2R^2/c^2$ vanishes; each frame is checked against the published metric, against $\rho = R$ and against $z = 2\sqrt{r_s(R - r_s)}$.
Melvin's universe is its plane $z = 0$ at one moment at $B = 1$, a vase whose circles grow to radius $1/B$ at the Melvin radius $\rho = 2/B$ and then narrow into a spike whose length grows as $B^2\rho^3/12$, drawn out to $\rho = 4/B$; Ernst's black hole is its equator at $t = 0$ at $B = 1/(2r_s)$, Flamm's throat opening to the widest circle at $r = 2/B = 4\,r_s$ and closing into the same kind of spike, on both sheets through the bifurcation sphere out to $r = 5\,r_s$, each circle checked against $\rho/\Lambda$ and $r/\Lambda$.
Ernst and Wild's Kerr hole in Melvin's universe is its equator at one moment of $t$ at $a = 4m/5$ and $B = 1/(4m)$, both sheets through the bifurcation sphere: a throat of radius $2mk/(1 + B^2m^2)$ at $r_+ = 8m/5$, the edge of the ergoregion at $1.942\,m$, the widest circle, of radius $k/B = 4.01\,m$, at $r = 7.950\,m$, and the surface narrowing beyond it as Melvin's does, drawn out to $r = 10\,m$.
Podolský and Ovcharenko's Kerr hole in Bertotti and Robinson's field is its equator at one moment of $t$ at $a = 4m/5$ and $B = 1/(4m)$, both sheets through the bifurcation sphere: a throat of radius $1.861\,m$ at $r_+$, the edge of the ergoregion at $2.021\,m$, and circles that widen toward the radius $3.838\,m$ as the surface turns upright, drawn out to $r = 50\,m$; $r \to \infty$ lies $7.560\,m$ from the throat.
Levi-Civita's cylinder is its plane $z = 0$ at one moment in Weyl's coordinates at $\sigma = 1/4$ and $C = 1$, the horn $z = (4\sqrt{\rho} - 1)^{3/2}/6$ with circles of radius $\sqrt{\rho}$, from $\rho = 1/16$, where it lies level, out to $\rho = 4$, both closed forms checked; inside $\rho = 1/16$ the circles grow faster than the distance out to them, which the view states and the script checks.
The Kantowski-Sachs family has two views, each moment's equator a flat cylinder of radius $b$ on which the stretch $|r| \le 1$ is $2a$ long, $\rho = b$ and $z = ar$ checked: the dust universe symmetric in time, $\kappa = 0$ and $b_0 = 1$, at five moments of its collapse from $\eta = 0$ to $1.2$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.03$ of $\eta$, and the inside of Schwarzschild's horizon at $T = 0.9$, $0.7$, $0.5$, $0.3$ and $0.1\,r_s$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.02\,r_s$ of $T$ whose `value` is the proper time since the horizon, so it runs at a steady proper time.
The two are two spacetimes of one line element, so each is marked on its own drawings alone, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
Robinson and Trautman's spacetime is a wave front, the surface of $\theta$ and $\phi$ at one $u$ and one $r$ of the axisymmetric chart, at $cu = 0$, $0.25$, $0.5$, $1$ and $2\,m$, played as a movie with a frame every $0.05\,m$ of $cu$ and the equator at $z = 0$ in every frame, with $f$ entering the slice as numbers from `null_rays.FrontSolver`: the fronts of one $u$ differ only in size, so each is drawn with its own $r$ as the unit, the first is $3.41\,r$ from pole to pole with an equator of radius $0.853\,r$, every one is checked against $\rho = r\sin\theta/f$ and to have the area $4\pi r^2$, and on the spacetime and conformal diagrams a moment is the whole outgoing ray $u = u_k$; `_tools/derivations/robinson_trautman.md` is the derivation.
The black hole threaded by a cosmic string, at $b = 0.9$ and $r_s = 1$, has two views, each belonging to every chart: the equator of the static chart at $t = 0$ through the throat, a circle of radius $br_s$, into a second exterior out to $6\,r_s$, checked against $\rho = br$ and $z = w\sqrt{1 + k^2w^2} + \mathrm{arsinh}(kw)/k$ with $w = \sqrt{r/r_s - 1}$ and $k = \sqrt{1 - b^2}$, which opens far out into the string's cone; and the horizon itself, the spindle $\rho = br_s\sin\theta$ with the apex of a cone at each pole, $d\rho/ds = b$ checked there, its length from pole to pole against $\pi r_s$ and its area against $4\pi br_s^2$.
Its bifurcation sphere lies off both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, so those drawings mark the equator alone, which `HIDDEN_VIEWS` in the tests records.
